- 2026-07-14 18:24 [job 167203] decoder_max_length sweep (161788 rate8, long buckets). 12k+: dml1024 f1=6.0 (100% trunc, 864 mems cut) / dml2048 f1=37.9 (13% trunc) / dml4096 f1=37.9 (0% trunc). 8-12k: 1024 f1=8.8(100%tr) / 2048=30.9(0tr) / 4096=30.9(0tr). Peak GPU 12k+: 1024=28.7GB, 2048=50.7GB, 4096=60.0GB. CONCLUSIONS: (1) too-small budget DESTROYS quality (1024 cuts ~half mems -> f1 collapses) = truncation harmful when SEVERE. (2) past 'just fits', MORE budget = FLAT f1 (2048->4096 = +0.0) but +9GB GPU -> removing the last 13% tail-truncation bought 0 f1 on multifieldqa (local extraction; tail didn't hold the answer). => 'just raise 2048' is a dead end beyond 'just enough'; cost scales with length, quality doesn't. Caveat: tail-truncation harmlessness is task-dependent - needle-in-haystack (answer IN tail) untested.
- 2026-07-14 18:28 FRAMING (recalibration): PISCO's core achievement = ~10-14x compression (rate8 7.5x / rate16 14.2x) with quality PRESERVED (semantic LLM-judge ties uncompressed ~0.58; LongBench matches uncompressed-9B). That bar is CLEARED and stands on its own. The decoder-budget/truncation ceiling is NOT a flaw in this - it only appears if pushing PISCO into a DIFFERENT, optional goal (arbitrary-length 50k-200k docs). For normal-length RAG/QA, current situation is GOOD. 'Any length' (fixed-MEM-budget / query-dependent / hierarchical) is an EXTENSION to pursue, not a hole to patch. Metric caveat: 'quality kept' holds under semantic correctness + LongBench, not strict F1/EM (=verbosity).

SURPRISE: PISCO NOT faster (online decode ratio 0.79-0.99, e2e 0.48-0.64 incl compress). NOT verbosity (outputs ~equal len, base slightly longer). CONFOUND FOUND: decoder is UNMERGED LoRA (lora_decoder r64; model.py PeftModel, no merge_and_unload) -> per-step adapter overhead. 0-4k diagnostic: identical 11.4-word output + tiny input both sides, yet PISCO +27% = fixed per-step overhead, not input-size compute. This microbench = near WORST case for PISCO speed (bs1, 32-tok out, moderate len<=14k so prefill savings small, LoRA unmerged, compression NOT cached). PISCO speed win lives in intended regime: cached MEMs (compress amortized) + long inputs (prefill-dominated) + batched throughput. CLEAN test needs: merge LoRA, decode-only, equal out-len, longer inputs/batch.
- 2026-07-14 20:17 [job 167209] OFFLINE-COMPRESSION speed (161788, cached compress=decode-only, LoRA MERGED, equal 32-tok output, both sdpa). Decode ms: base 819/886/948/1065 (grows w/ length), PISCO ~FLAT 1363/1325/1340/1337. SPEEDUP base/pisco = 0.60/0.67/0.71/0.80x -> PISCO STILL SLOWER at <=14k, 32-tok out. WHY: 32 autoregressive steps dominate wall-clock; PISCO's smaller-prefill edge is a thin slice here; PISCO decode ~constant regardless of mem count (371->1832) => dominated by fixed step overhead + inputs_embeds generation-path overhead (NOT mem-compute). KEY TREND: base grows w/ length, PISCO flat -> gap narrows 0.60->0.80x -> extrapolated CROSSOVER ~25-30k input tokens where PISCO decode becomes faster. So PISCO decode-latency win needs LONGER inputs (bigger prefill gap) and/or LONGER outputs (per-step smaller-KV advantage compounds). Part of deficit = fixable inputs_embeds overhead, not fundamental. Orthogonal: throughput/batching (smaller KV=more concurrent req) not measured here (bs=1).
- 2026-07-15 09:18 [jobs 167214/167273] THROUGHPUT reproduces PISCO paper Table 3. Batched decode, precomputed MEMs, 5x128 ctx, 32-tok out. 4B/rate8: bs1=1.00x -> bs256=2.28x; PISCO max-batch 512 vs base 256. 9B/rate16(x16): bs1=1.01x -> bs64=2.17x -> bs256=3.66x (52.8 vs 14.4 seq/s); PISCO fits >=1024, base OOM@512 => >=4x max-batch (=paper's '4x larger batch' lever). RESOLUTION: PISCO NOT slow. Efficiency = THROUGHPUT+max-batch, ~1x at bs=1 (no single-req latency win, expected), grows with batch/model-size/compression. Paper's 5.7x = Mistral-7B + x16/x128 + true max batch; we hit 3.66x at Qwen9B/x16 - same scaling law. Earlier '0.6-0.8x slower' was wrong-regime artifact (bs=1, long single doc, mismatched separately-loaded base). New tooling: scripts/bench_throughput.py + eval_agent_qa timing/merge_lora/min_new_tokens instrumentation.
- 2026-07-15 11:29 [job 167291] DECODE PROFILE (9B/149907, 32-tok gen, module hooks+CUDA events). bs=1: attention 12.9% / FFN 19.3% / LM-head(248k vocab) 1.7% / framework-overhead 66.1%. bs=64: attn 11.5% / FFN 22.2% / LM-head 7.2% / overhead 59.1%. CORRECTS earlier vocab claim: LM-head is 2-7% = NEGLIGIBLE for per-step compute (I was wrong to emphasize 248k vocab). KEY: attention (ONLY part PISCO reduces) is ~12% -> at bs=1/short-out PISCO capped at ~1.14x (=why bs1 showed 1.0x). 66% is framework/inputs_embeds overhead (input-independent). PISCO's 2.6-3.7x at bs256 comes from: overhead AMORTIZES over batch + attention/KV scales with batch x len (base 5x more tokens) -> attention dominates at high batch + base OOMs. Vocab helps PISCO only via MEMORY/max-batch (fat vocab OOMs sooner), NOT per-step speed. Real ratio levers: long context + long output + high batch.

Design review of coarse_to_fine_order (owner question 2026-11-04): bit-reversal starts at 0, so the first kept slot sits at the START of the chunk (n=8: 6%; n=33: order 0,32,16,8,24 = start, end, middle, quarters), not at the middle as the PISCOConfig docstring claims; a position id gives a slot a point not a width, so no order yields a true coarse level (whole chunk / halves / quarters) without nested-budget training (drop lower levels for the decoder at random) and probably a level marker. The running archq3b chain only tested 'does the permutation hurt at full slots'. DECISION: coarse stage 2 (184706, killed by the outage) is NOT relaunched; archq3b parked. Next on this thread, probe first: on the spread finetune, keep only the first k slots at inference and measure answer NLL/judge (how much any prefix covers today), and permute slot order on the decoder side (does the decoder use within-chunk order on a spread model? the 'order unused' finding came from the tail model).
